    "originalFile": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe validate through Monte Carlo simulation ($K{=}3$), cross-$K$ robustness checks ($K{=}5,7$), and empirical validation with real LLM confusion matrices from three natural language processing (NLP) tasks ($K{=}2,3,10$).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nFor each topology, we sample 100{,}000 confusion matrices from a Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ prior, compute analytical probability limits, and validate against Monte Carlo simulation ($N{=}10{,}000$). ASV experiments use $C(\\delta) = (1{-}\\delta)I + \\delta C_0$.\n\n\\subsection{Bias Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy_results}\n\n\\paragraph{Theory-MC agreement.}\nAnalytical probability limits match Monte Carlo estimates with mean absolute percentage error (MAPE) of 1.6\\%, averaged across 161 scenarios (23 LLM configurations $\\times$ 7 topologies; per-topology range: 0.3\\%--3.7\\%), validating the closed-form derivations and justifying probability limits as practical bias diagnostics.\n\n\\paragraph{Exposure DAG: sign reversal under multi-class misclassification.}\nUnder realistic accuracy (diagonal $\\geq 0.7$), the violation rate is 13.7\\% with 2.1\\% sign reversal; at diagonal $\\geq 0.9$, violation persists at 11.9\\% (Appendix~\\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}). Under the full Dirichlet prior, 85\\% of matrices violate the binary ``toward-null'' guarantee and 83.4\\% cause sign reversal; $\\min_k C_{kk} < 0.55$ predicts sign reversal with 91\\% accuracy (Appendix~\\ref{sec:sign_determinants}). The $K{=}2$ control confirms classical attenuation~\\cite{brenner1993}. This establishes the multi-class failure as structurally distinct.\n\n\\paragraph{Structural guarantee for non-exposure topologies.}\nFive topologies exhibit zero sign-flip rate (Table~\\ref{tab:taxonomy}): confounding, mediation, and front-door show 100\\% amplification~\\cite{brenner1993, dosemeci1990} but never reverse the sign; collider is protective ($|B(C)|$ non-increasing; Conjecture~\\ref{conj:monotone}); M-bias shows weakest sensitivity (53.2\\% violation). All patterns persist at $K{=}5,7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:cross_k_robustness}).\n\n\\paragraph{IV: Wald estimator instability.}\nThe IV topology is the sole non-exposure exception: 52.6\\% of random matrices cause sign reversal, stable across $K{=}3,5,7$. Diagonal dominance ($C_{kk} > 0.5$) eliminates sign reversal but preserves amplification (Appendix~\\ref{sec:iv_dirichlet_subspace}); however, all 23 empirical LLM configurations show zero IV sign reversal.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Bias under $K{=}3$ non-differential misclassification (100{,}000 Dirichlet$(1,1,1)$ matrices). Violation: $|\\text{bias}| > 0$; Sign Flip: any coefficient reverses sign; Amplif.: $|\\hat{\\tau}| > |\\tau|$. $\\tau_{\\text{sf}}{=}0$?: sign-flip guarantee holds unconditionally (Yes), conditionally (Cond.; Proposition~\\ref{prop:signflip}), or not (No). Collider 100\\% amplification reflects Berkson bias; misclassification is protective.}\n\\label{tab:taxonomy}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Topology} & \\textbf{Violation} & \\textbf{Sign Flip} & \\textbf{Amplif.} & $\\tau_{\\text{sf}}{=}0$? \\\\\n\\midrule\nConfounding & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & Cond. \\\\\nExposure & 85\\% & 83.4\\% & 14.1\\% & No \\\\\nMediation & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & Cond. \\\\\nFront-door & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & Cond. \\\\\nCollider & 100\\% & 0\\% & 100\\% & Cond. \\\\\nM-bias & 53.2\\% & 0\\% & 10.4\\% & Yes \\\\\nIV & 100\\% & 52.6\\% & 100\\% & No \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\nFigure~\\ref{fig:dirichlet_overlay} overlays all 23 empirical LLM configurations onto the theoretical violation-rate curves; real-world accuracy ($\\min_k C_{kk} \\geq 0.6$) places all models in the low-risk region for IV but leaves exposure vulnerable.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig7_dirichlet_overlay.pdf}\n\\caption{Empirical LLM confusion matrices (markers) overlaid on theoretical violation-rate curves from $K{=}3$ Dirichlet analysis. Left: exposure; right: IV. Real-world accuracy places all models in the low-risk region for IV sign reversal but not for exposure.}\n\\label{fig:dirichlet_overlay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig5_f1_vs_bias.pdf}\n\\caption{Topology determines downstream causal bias, not $F_1$. HumAID $K{=}10$ (56 points): within $F_1$ range 0.614--0.690, bias varies $10{\\times}$ (IV 42.1\\% vs.\\ collider $<$5.6\\%).}\n\\label{fig:f1_vs_bias}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{ASV Framework Validation}\n\\label{sec:asv_results}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{ASV summary across topologies ($K{=}3$, $\\tau{=}0.5$). ---: not applicable.}\n\\label{tab:asv_summary}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Topology} & \\textbf{Mag ASV} & \\textbf{Power} & \\textbf{Sign-flip} \\\\\n\\midrule\nIV          & 0.10--0.61 & 0\\%   & 1.1--6.7 \\\\\nFront-door  & 0.06--0.39 & 100\\% & --- \\\\\nConfounding & 0.08--0.49 & 100\\% & --- \\\\\nMediation   & 0.08--0.45 & 0\\%   & --- \\\\\nCollider    & 0.34--2.03 & 0\\%   & --- \\\\\nM-bias      & $\\infty$   & 98\\%  & --- \\\\\nExposure    & ---        & ---   & 83.4\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{ASV results.}\nThe ASV envelope is $\\varepsilon$-approximately monotonic ($\\varepsilon < 0.02$; Conjecture~\\ref{conj:monotone}); IV is maximally fragile, M-bias is immune ($\\text{ASV} = \\infty$; Figure~\\ref{fig:asv_ranking}). Sign-flip ASV: 83.4\\% of exposure matrices yield $\\delta^*_{\\text{sf}} < 1$; IV ranges 1.1--6.7 (Table~\\ref{tab:asv_summary}). Significance ASV exhibits a phase transition at $N^* \\approx 6{,}000$--$10{,}000$; bootstrap CIs (Appendix~\\ref{sec:bootstrap_asv},~\\ref{sec:c_robustness}) confirm stable topology ranking, with the safe/fragile partition recovering from $n_{\\text{gold}} \\geq 100$ (82\\% stability; Appendix~\\ref{sec:asv_diagnostics},~\\ref{sec:asv_case_study}).\n\n\\subsection{Empirical Validation}\n\\label{sec:empirical}\n\nWe validate using real LLM confusion matrices from HumAID~\\cite{alam2021humaid} ($K{=}10$), VAST ($K{=}3$), and CivilComments ($K{=}2$): four LLMs, 23 configurations, 161 bias scenarios. HumAID ($K{=}10$) matches predictions: IV (12.4--42.1\\%) and exposure (9.0--17.4\\%) dominate; remaining topologies $<$15\\%. $F_1$ does not exhibit significant rank correlation with bias ($\\rho = -0.10$, $p = 0.45$; Figure~\\ref{fig:f1_vs_bias}), but per-topology patterns diverge ($\\rho \\leq -0.53$ for five topologies vs.\\ $0.87$ for M-bias; Appendix~\\ref{sec:within_topo_f1}). Of exposure coefficients, 14/23 (61\\%) exhibit sign reversal, all secondary; zero across 138 non-exposure scenarios.\n\n\\subsection{Correction Guidance}\n\\label{sec:correction}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig4_binarization.pdf}\n\\caption{Binarization collapse $\\Delta$: additional bias from collapsing $K{=}3$ to binary. Topology determines severity ($>$150$\\times$ range); exposure and IV exhibit sign flips.}\n\\label{fig:binarization}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe evaluate five correction methods (naive plug-in, maximum likelihood adjustment (MLA), design-based supervised learning (DSL)~\\cite{egami_hinck_stewart_wei2023}, Monte Carlo simulation extrapolation (MC-SIMEX)~\\cite{kuchenhoff2006simex}, PPI++~\\cite{angelopoulos2023}) across all topologies (210 configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:correction}, Appendix~\\ref{app:supplementary}).\n\n\\paragraph{FWL invariance.}\nUnder linear regression, FWL invariance renders matrix-based methods (naive plug-in, MLA) provably ineffective for covariate topologies (0\\% bias reduction; Appendix~\\ref{app:fwl_proof}): the treatment coefficient depends only on the projection orthogonal to the covariate space, so relabeling $A^* \\to A$ leaves the coefficient unchanged. Under logistic regression this invariance breaks, but the method ranking persists ($|\\Delta| < 0.02$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:fwl_nonlinear}).\n\n\\paragraph{Method ranking and decision procedure.}\nPPI++ achieves the highest root mean squared error (RMSE) reduction (47.0\\% at $K{=}3$) but requires gold labels (Appendix~\\ref{sec:ppi_gold_ratio}) and harms M-bias ($-$88\\%); MC-SIMEX is gold-free and $K$-robust (27--70\\%), though it harms front-door ($-$62.4\\%); rankings are stable across $K$ ($\\rho \\geq 0.80$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:correction_k_sensitivity}). Algorithm~\\ref{alg:correction} (Appendix) routes through risk assessment, method selection, and FWL screening. End-to-end demos (Appendix~\\ref{sec:e2e_demo},~\\ref{sec:real_cm_demo}; Appendix~\\ref{app:case_study}) validate: PPI++ reduces exposure bias from 42.9\\% to 0.2\\%, while topology-agnostic IV correction amplifies bias ($-$139\\%). Collapsing $K{=}3$ to binary is not a safe fallback: the additional bias $\\Delta$ varies $>$150$\\times$ by topology (Figure~\\ref{fig:binarization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:binarization}).\n",
